1	use std::hint::black_box;
2	
3	use hdrhistogram::Histogram;
4	
5	use crate::overhead::Overhead;
6	
7	const WARMUP: u64 = 10_000;
8	const ESTIMATE_STEPS: u64 = 1_000;
9	const ESTIMATE_SAMPLES: usize = 5;
10	const FRAMING_DOMINATION_RATIO: f64 = 10.0;
11	const MAX_INNER: u64 = 1_000;
12	const MIN_ITERATIONS: u64 = 1_000;
13	const MAX_ITERATIONS: u64 = 1_000_000_000;
14	
15	pub trait Bench {
16	    fn name(&self) -> &str;
17	    fn step(&mut self) -> u64;
18	}
19	
20	pub struct RunCfg<'a> {
21	    pub overhead: &'a Overhead,
22	    pub target_seconds: f64,
23	    pub iterations_override: Option<u64>,
24	    pub inner_override: Option<u64>,
25	}
26	
27	pub fn run_adaptive<B: Bench>(bench: &mut B, cfg: &RunCfg) -> (Histogram<u64>, u64, u64) {
28	    for _ in 0..WARMUP {
29	        black_box(bench.step());
30	    }
31	
32	    let step_cost_ns = estimate_step_cost(bench);
33	    let framing_ns = cfg.overhead.framing_per_sample_ns.max(1.0);
34	    let inner = cfg
35	        .inner_override
36	        .unwrap_or_else(|| pick_inner(step_cost_ns, framing_ns));
37	    let iterations = cfg
38	        .iterations_override
39	        .unwrap_or_else(|| pick_iterations(step_cost_ns, inner, cfg.target_seconds));
40	
41	    let hist = run_bench(bench, iterations, inner);
42	    (hist, iterations, inner)
43	}
44	
45	fn estimate_step_cost<B: Bench>(bench: &mut B) -> f64 {
46	    let mut samples: Vec<f64> = (0..ESTIMATE_SAMPLES)
47	        .map(|_| {
48	            let start = minstant::Instant::now();
49	            for _ in 0..ESTIMATE_STEPS {
50	                black_box(bench.step());
51	            }
52	            let total = start.elapsed().as_nanos() as u64;
53	            total as f64 / ESTIMATE_STEPS as f64
54	        })
55	        .collect();
56	    samples.sort_by(|a, b| a.partial_cmp(b).unwrap());
57	    samples[ESTIMATE_SAMPLES / 2]
58	}
59	
60	fn pick_inner(step_cost_ns: f64, framing_ns: f64) -> u64 {
61	    let target = (FRAMING_DOMINATION_RATIO * framing_ns / step_cost_ns).ceil() as u64;
62	    target.clamp(1, MAX_INNER)
63	}
64	
65	fn pick_iterations(step_cost_ns: f64, inner: u64, target_seconds: f64) -> u64 {
66	    let target_ns = target_seconds * 1e9;
67	    let per_sample_ns = inner as f64 * step_cost_ns;
68	    let raw = (target_ns / per_sample_ns).ceil() as u64;
69	    raw.clamp(MIN_ITERATIONS, MAX_ITERATIONS)
70	}
71	
72	fn run_bench<B: Bench>(bench: &mut B, iterations: u64, inner: u64) -> Histogram<u64> {
73	    // 1 ns to 60 s, 3 sig figs
74	    let mut hist = Histogram::<u64>::new_with_bounds(1, 60_000_000_000, 3).unwrap();
75	
76	    for _ in 0..iterations {
77	        let start = minstant::Instant::now();
78	        for _ in 0..inner {
79	            black_box(bench.step());
80	        }
81	        let elapsed_ns = start.elapsed().as_nanos() as u64;
82	        hist.record(round_elapsed(elapsed_ns, inner)).unwrap();
83	    }
84	
85	    hist
86	}
87	
88	fn round_elapsed(elapsed_ns: u64, inner: u64) -> u64 {
89	    (elapsed_ns + inner / 2) / inner
90	}
91	
92	pub fn fmt_commas(n: u64) -> String {
93	    let s = n.to_string();
94	    let mut result = String::new();
95	    for (i, c) in s.chars().rev().enumerate() {
96	        if i > 0 && i % 3 == 0 {
97	            result.push(',');
98	        }
99	        result.push(c);
100	    }
101	    result.chars().rev().collect()
102	}
103	
104	pub fn fmt_commas_f64(n: f64, decimals: usize) -> String {
105	    let s = format!("{n:.decimals$}");
106	    let (sign, body) = match s.strip_prefix('-') {
107	        Some(rest) => ("-", rest),
108	        None => ("", s.as_str()),
109	    };
110	    let (int_part, frac_part) = match body.find('.') {
111	        Some(i) => (&body[..i], &body[i..]),
112	        None => (body, ""),
113	    };
114	    let int_num: u64 = int_part.parse().unwrap_or(0);
115	    format!("{sign}{}{frac_part}", fmt_commas(int_num))
116	}
117	
118	pub fn print_histogram(
119	    name: &str,
120	    iterations: u64,
121	    inner: u64,
122	    hist: &Histogram<u64>,
123	    overhead: &Overhead,
124	) {
125	    let adj = overhead.per_call_ns(inner);
126	    let total = iterations * inner;
127	    println!(
128	        "{name} ({} iters × {} inner = {} calls; subtract {} ns/call)",
129	        fmt_commas(iterations),
130	        inner,
131	        fmt_commas(total),
132	        fmt_commas_f64(adj, 2),
133	    );
134	    println!("                      raw      adjusted");
135	    print_row("min", hist.min(), adj);
136	    print_row("p1", hist.value_at_quantile(0.01), adj);
137	    print_row("p10", hist.value_at_quantile(0.10), adj);
138	    print_row("p50", hist.value_at_quantile(0.50), adj);
139	    print_row("p90", hist.value_at_quantile(0.90), adj);
140	    print_row("p99", hist.value_at_quantile(0.99), adj);
141	    print_row("p99.9", hist.value_at_quantile(0.999), adj);
142	    print_row("p99.99", hist.value_at_quantile(0.9999), adj);
143	    print_row("max", hist.max(), adj);
144	    let mean = hist.mean();
145	    let adj_mean = (mean - adj).max(0.0);
146	    println!(
147	        "  mean       {:>10} ns    {:>10} ns",
148	        fmt_commas_f64(mean, 1),
149	        fmt_commas_f64(adj_mean, 1)
150	    );
151	    println!("  stdev      {:>10} ns", fmt_commas_f64(hist.stdev(), 1));
152	    println!();
153	}
154	
155	fn print_row(label: &str, raw: u64, overhead: f64) {
156	    let adj = (raw as f64 - overhead).max(0.0);
157	    println!(
158	        "  {label:<8} {:>10} ns    {:>10} ns",
159	        fmt_commas(raw),
160	        fmt_commas_f64(adj, 1)
161	    );
162	}
163	
```

1	# Notes
2	
3	This directory contains various notes and documentation related to the project.
4	Each file is organized by topic for easy reference.
5	
6	By default there are chores-*.md and todo.md. Chores are general notes
7	about tasks and todo.md contains short term tasks and their status.
8	
9	In the future we I expect we may want to create a "notes"
10	database to better manage the information, TBD.
11	
12	Examples chore file:
13	```
14	# Chores-01.md
15	
16	General maintenance tasks and considerations for the project see other files for
17	more specific topics. A chore in a chores file provides quick information on the
18	how and why of a particular chore. The section header is short and sweet
19	and the title is appended with the version number of the app when the chore
20	is completed.
21	
22	## Create an app that does something interesting (0.1.0)
23	
24	The app counts from 1 to 100, not to interesting.
25	```
26	
27	## Commit Workflow
28	
29	This project uses a per-step commit / push / finalize flow against
30	two paired repos (app + bot session). Full rules live in
31	[CLAUDE.md](../CLAUDE.md#commit-push-finalize-flow) — that file is
32	the source of truth (the bot loads it automatically; humans can
33	read it from there too).
34	
35	## jj tips
36	
37	For users new to jj see [jj-tips.md](jj-tips.md).
38	
39	```
40	## Chores format
41	
42	Filename: "chores-XX.md"
43	example: chores-01.md
44	
45	Format of section labels: "## <short description> (X.Y.Z)"
46	example: "## Topic format description (0.1.0)"
47	
48	Example chore file:
49	```
50	# Chores-01.md
51	 
52	General maintenance tasks and considerations for the project see other files for
53	more specific topics. A chore in a chores file provides quick information on the
54	how and why of a particular chore.
55	
56	## Do something (1.3.1)
57	
58	Describe something
59	```
60	
61	## Versioning during development
62	
63	This is using jujustiu, jj + git and we'll see how it goes. Below is my
64	git workflow, jj will be different but we'll have to discover that as
65	we go.
66	
67	Every plan must start with a version bump. Choose the approach based on scope:
68	
69	- **Single-step** (recommended for mechanical/focused changes): bump directly to
70	  `X.Y.Z`, implement in one commit. Simpler history.
71	- **Multi-step** (for exploratory/large changes): bump to `X.Y.Z-devN`, implement
72	  across multiple commits, final commit removes `-devN`.
73	
74	The plan should recommend one approach and get user approval before starting.
75	
76	For multi-step:
77	1. Bump version to `X.Y.Z-devN` with a plan and commit as a chore marker
78	2. Implement in one or more `-devN` commits (bump N as needed)
79	3. Final commit removes `-devN`, updates todo/chores — this is the "done" marker
80	
81	The final release commit (without `-devN`) signals completion rather than amending
82	prior commits. This keeps the git history readable and makes it easy to see which
83	commits were exploratory vs final.
84	
85	## Todo format
86	
87	Todo.md contains two main sections "Todo" and "Done" each item is a
88	short explanations of a tasks and links to more details using 1 or more
89	references.
90	
91	Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
92	In markdown, `[2,3]` is a single ref key (won't resolve) and `[2][3]`
93	is parsed as display text `2` with ref key `3` (so `[2]` won't resolve).
94	
95	Examples:
96	
97	# Todo
98	
99	- Add new feature X [details](chores-01.md#feature-x)
100	- Fix bug Y [1]
101	
102	# Done
103	
104	- Fixed issue Z [2],[3]
105	
106	[1]: chores-01bugs.md#bug-y
107	
```

> AGENT

Quick approach before I touch code:

**Change**: time the benchmark loop in `run_bench`, return the elapsed duration from `run_adaptive`, display it in the header line.

**Format** — which do you prefer?
- A: `(1,131,278 iters × 1 inner = 1,131,278 calls in 7.99 s; subtract 11.60 ns/call)`
- B: `(1,131,278 iters × 1 inner = 1,131,278 calls; 7.99 s; subtract 11.60 ns/call)`
- C: trailing — `...; subtract 11.60 ns/call; 7.99 s)`

**Versioning**: single-step bump 0.3.1 → 0.3.2 (mechanical, small). OK?

> DEVELOPER

I'd like to to be something like:
std::sync::mpsc round-trip (2 threads) duration: 10.1s; iters: 1,131,278 × 1 inner; calls: 1,131,278 subtract 11.60 ns/call)

> AGENT

Confirming before I code:

Final format (adding the missing `;` for consistency):
```
std::sync::mpsc round-trip (2 threads) duration: 10.1s; iters: 1,131,278 × 1 inner; calls: 1,131,278; subtract 11.60 ns/call
```

- no wrapping parens
- duration as `10.1s` (1 decimal, seconds)
- version: single-step 0.3.1 → 0.3.2

OK to proceed?

> DEVELOPER

or maybe with '='
std::sync::mpsc round-trip (2 threads) duration=10.1s; iters=1,131,278; inner=1; calls=1,131,278; subtract=11.60 ns/call;
thoughts

> AGENT

I like it — `key=value` pairs are cleaner, self-describing, and scan well. Each field stands alone so reordering/adding is trivial.

Two small thoughts:
- Drop the trailing `;` — it's usually treated as a separator, not terminator. Final line would be: `...; subtract=11.60 ns/call`
- `subtract=11.60 ns/call` mixes `=` with a unit phrase; still reads fine though.

Proposed:
```
std::sync::mpsc round-trip (2 threads) duration=10.1s; iters=1,131,278; inner=1; calls=1,131,278; subtract=11.60 ns/call
```

OK to proceed (single-step 0.3.1 → 0.3.2)?

> DEVELOPER

or
std::sync::mpsc round-trip (2 threads) [duration=10.1s iters=1,131,278 inner=1 calls=1,131,278 subtract=11.60ns/call]
this an array of key=value separated by spaces?

> AGENT

Yes — that's logfmt-style, widely used (Heroku, Go's log/slog, lots of ops tooling). Pros:

- Brackets visually separate metadata from the name
- Space-separated is easier to eyeball than `;`-separated
- Parses trivially with any logfmt lib
- No punctuation ambiguity

Only nit: `11.60ns/call` (no space) vs `11.60 ns/call` (with space). No-space keeps each token atomic, which is more logfmt-y and avoids the "is this one value or two" question. Go with no-space.

Final:
```
std::sync::mpsc round-trip (2 threads) [duration=10.1s iters=1,131,278 inner=1 calls=1,131,278 subtract=11.60ns/call]
```

OK to proceed (single-step 0.3.1 → 0.3.2)?

> DEVELOPER

[duration=10.1s outer=1,131,278 inner=1 calls=1,131,278 adj/call=11.60ns]

> AGENT

Clean — `outer`/`inner` pair nicely, and `adj/call` is more descriptive than `subtract`.

Final:
```
std::sync::mpsc round-trip (2 threads) [duration=10.1s outer=1,131,278 inner=1 calls=1,131,278 adj/call=11.60ns]
```

Shall I proceed with single-step 0.3.1 → 0.3.2?

> DEVELOPER

y

1	mod benches;
2	mod harness;
3	mod overhead;
4	
5	use clap::Parser;
6	
7	#[derive(Parser)]
8	#[command(version, about = "IIAC performance measurement", max_term_width = 80)]
9	struct Cli {
10	    /// Benches to run. Pass 'all' for every registered bench, or
11	    /// one or more names. Run with no args to see the available list.
12	    benches: Vec<String>,
13	
14	    /// Target wall-clock seconds per bench (default 5.0). Auto-sizes
15	    /// iterations and INNER. Mutually exclusive with -D.
16	    #[arg(short = 'd', long, conflicts_with = "total_duration")]
17	    duration: Option<f64>,
18	
19	    /// Target total wall-clock seconds across all requested benches;
20	    /// budget is split equally per bench. Mutually exclusive with -d.
21	    #[arg(short = 'D', long)]
22	    total_duration: Option<f64>,
23	
24	    /// Override iterations (skips auto-sizing of total count; INNER still adapts).
25	    #[arg(short, long)]
26	    iterations: Option<u64>,
27	
28	    /// Override INNER (skips auto-sizing of inner-loop count).
29	    /// INNER=1 measures single-call latency (each sample = one step); higher
30	    /// INNER measures back-to-back/burst rate (each sample = N steps averaged).
31	    #[arg(short = 'I', long)]
32	    inner: Option<u64>,
33	}
34	
35	const DEFAULT_DURATION: f64 = 5.0;
36	
37	fn main() {
38	    let cli = Cli::parse();
39	
40	    if cli.benches.is_empty() {
41	        println!("no benches specified. use -h or --help for more info.");
42	        println!("available: all, {}", benches::names().join(", "));
43	        return;
44	    }
45	
46	    println!(
47	        "iiac-perf {} — IIAC performance measurement\n",
48	        env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION")
49	    );
50	
51	    let overhead = overhead::calibrate();
52	    println!("calibration:");
53	    println!(
54	        "  framing/sample    {:>7} ns  (timer pair, two-point fit)",
55	        harness::fmt_commas_f64(overhead.framing_per_sample_ns, 2)
56	    );
57	    println!(
58	        "  loop/iter         {:>7} ns  (per inner-loop iteration)",
59	        harness::fmt_commas_f64(overhead.loop_per_iter_ns, 2)
60	    );
61	    println!();
62	
63	    let runners = match benches::resolve(&cli.benches) {
64	        Ok(r) => r,
65	        Err(e) => {
66	            eprintln!("error: {e}");
67	            std::process::exit(2);
68	        }
69	    };
70	
71	    let target_seconds = match (cli.duration, cli.total_duration) {
72	        (Some(d), _) => d,
73	        (None, Some(t)) => t / runners.len() as f64,
74	        (None, None) => DEFAULT_DURATION,
75	    };
76	
77	    let cfg = harness::RunCfg {
78	        overhead: &overhead,
79	        target_seconds,
80	        iterations_override: cli.iterations,
81	        inner_override: cli.inner,
82	    };
83	
84	    for run in runners {
85	        run(&cfg);
86	    }
87	}
88	
```

1	use std::hint::black_box;
2	use std::sync::mpsc;
3	use std::thread;
4	
5	use crate::harness::{self, Bench, RunCfg};
6	
7	pub const NAME: &str = "mpsc-2t";
8	
9	pub struct StdMpsc2Thread {
10	    req_tx: mpsc::Sender<u64>,
11	    resp_rx: mpsc::Receiver<u64>,
12	    worker: Option<thread::JoinHandle<()>>,
13	    counter: u64,
14	}
15	
16	impl StdMpsc2Thread {
17	    pub fn new() -> Self {
18	        let (req_tx, req_rx) = mpsc::channel::<u64>();
19	        let (resp_tx, resp_rx) = mpsc::channel::<u64>();
20	        let worker = thread::spawn(move || {
21	            while let Ok(v) = req_rx.recv() {
22	                if resp_tx.send(v).is_err() {
23	                    break;
24	                }
25	            }
26	        });
27	        Self {
28	            req_tx,
29	            resp_rx,
30	            worker: Some(worker),
31	            counter: 0,
32	        }
33	    }
34	}
35	
36	impl Bench for StdMpsc2Thread {
37	    fn name(&self) -> &str {
38	        "std::sync::mpsc round-trip (2 threads)"
39	    }
40	
41	    fn step(&mut self) -> u64 {
42	        self.counter = self.counter.wrapping_add(1);
43	        self.req_tx.send(self.counter).unwrap();
44	        let v = self.resp_rx.recv().unwrap();
45	        black_box(v)
46	    }
47	}
48	
49	impl Drop for StdMpsc2Thread {
50	    fn drop(&mut self) {
51	        // Replace req_tx with a dummy so we can drop the real one;
52	        // worker's recv() then returns Err and the worker exits.
53	        // Done this way (instead of Option<Sender>) to keep step() branch-free.
54	        let (dummy_tx, _) = mpsc::channel();
55	        drop(std::mem::replace(&mut self.req_tx, dummy_tx));
56	        if let Some(worker) = self.worker.take() {
57	            let _ = worker.join();
58	        }
59	    }
60	}
61	
62	pub fn run(cfg: &RunCfg) {
63	    let mut bench = StdMpsc2Thread::new();
64	    let (hist, iterations, inner) = harness::run_adaptive(&mut bench, cfg);
65	    harness::print_histogram(bench.name(), iterations, inner, &hist, cfg.overhead);
66	}
67	
```

## Tune duration default + add total-duration flag (0.3.1)

Empirical follow-up to 0.3.0. User found `-d 60` gives ~0.3 % run-to-run
mean swing on mpsc-2t vs ~4 % at `-d 1`, but no data points in between.

### Why

A reasonable default `-d` should give "good enough" stability without
forcing the user to wait long. Also, the current `-d` is per-bench;
some workflows want a fixed total wall-clock budget split across the
requested benches.

### Dev steps

1. `0.3.1-dev1` ✅ measured mpsc-2t at `-d` ∈ {1, 3, 5, 10, 30} × 3
   runs on 3900x. Picked **default = 5.0** at the knee.

   | -d | mean range (ns) | mean swing | p50 range | p99 range |
   |----|----------------|-----------|-----------|-----------|
   | 1  | 7,377–8,890    | ~18 %     | 7,075–8,847 | 11,047–12,343 |
   | 3  | 7,399–8,577    | ~15 %     | 7,263–8,847 | 10,775–11,695 |
   | 5  | 6,960–7,611    | **~9 %**  | 6,763–7,363 | 10,319–11,191 |
   | 10 | 7,132–8,072    | ~12 %     | 6,843–8,199 | 10,703–11,583 |
   | 30 | 7,467–7,711    | ~3 %      | 7,295–7,383 | 10,895–11,127 |

   d=5 is the knee: p50 range collapses 1,772→600 ns, mean swing
   nearly halves over d=1. d=10 didn't pay back its 2× cost in
   this sample (3 runs is small for inference; d=10 would likely
   stabilise with more). d=30 is gold-standard but 2 minutes for
   `iiac-perf all` is too long for a default. d=5 → ~25 s for `all`,
   tolerable.

   Default bumped from 1.0 to 5.0 in `main.rs`. Users wanting
   publication-grade stability use `-d 30` explicitly; README notes
   this.
2. `0.3.1-dev2` ✅ added `-D/--total-duration SECONDS`. Splits the
   budget equally across requested benches; mutually exclusive with
   `-d` via clap `conflicts_with` (clap exits with a friendly error
   if both are passed). `-d` is now `Option<f64>` so `conflicts_with`
   works correctly without faking explicit set; default falls back to
   `DEFAULT_DURATION = 5.0` only when neither flag is given.
   `/README.md` Usage updated with the new flag and an `-D 30`
   example.

   Also fixed `iiac-perf -h` not wrapping long descriptions: clap
   needs the `wrap_help` cargo feature (not in default features) to
   actually wrap, plus `max_term_width = 80` in `#[command(...)]`
   to cap on wide terminals.
3. `0.3.1` ✅ finalize: drop `-devN`, move todo entry to Done.
```

  "old_string": "pub fn run_adaptive<B: Bench>(bench: &mut B, cfg: &RunCfg) -> (Histogram<u64>, u64, u64) {\n    for _ in 0..WARMUP {\n        black_box(bench.step());\n    }\n\n    let step_cost_ns = estimate_step_cost(bench);\n    let framing_ns = cfg.overhead.framing_per_sample_ns.max(1.0);\n    let inner = cfg\n        .inner_override\n        .unwrap_or_else(|| pick_inner(step_cost_ns, framing_ns));\n    let iterations = cfg\n        .iterations_override\n        .unwrap_or_else(|| pick_iterations(step_cost_ns, inner, cfg.target_seconds));\n\n    let hist = run_bench(bench, iterations, inner);\n    (hist, iterations, inner)\n}",
  "new_string": "pub fn run_adaptive<B: Bench>(bench: &mut B, cfg: &RunCfg) -> (Histogram<u64>, u64, u64, f64) {\n    for _ in 0..WARMUP {\n        black_box(bench.step());\n    }\n\n    let step_cost_ns = estimate_step_cost(bench);\n    let framing_ns = cfg.overhead.framing_per_sample_ns.max(1.0);\n    let inner = cfg\n        .inner_override\n        .unwrap_or_else(|| pick_inner(step_cost_ns, framing_ns));\n    let iterations = cfg\n        .iterations_override\n        .unwrap_or_else(|| pick_iterations(step_cost_ns, inner, cfg.target_seconds));\n\n    let (hist, duration_s) = run_bench(bench, iterations, inner);\n    (hist, iterations, inner, duration_s)\n}"
}

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/harness.rs",
  "old_string": "fn run_bench<B: Bench>(bench: &mut B, iterations: u64, inner: u64) -> Histogram<u64> {\n    // 1 ns to 60 s, 3 sig figs\n    let mut hist = Histogram::<u64>::new_with_bounds(1, 60_000_000_000, 3).unwrap();\n\n    for _ in 0..iterations {\n        let start = minstant::Instant::now();\n        for _ in 0..inner {\n            black_box(bench.step());\n        }\n        let elapsed_ns = start.elapsed().as_nanos() as u64;\n        hist.record(round_elapsed(elapsed_ns, inner)).unwrap();\n    }\n\n    hist\n}",
  "new_string": "fn run_bench<B: Bench>(bench: &mut B, iterations: u64, inner: u64) -> (Histogram<u64>, f64) {\n    // 1 ns to 60 s, 3 sig figs\n    let mut hist = Histogram::<u64>::new_with_bounds(1, 60_000_000_000, 3).unwrap();\n\n    let run_start = minstant::Instant::now();\n    for _ in 0..iterations {\n        let start = minstant::Instant::now();\n        for _ in 0..inner {\n            black_box(bench.step());\n        }\n        let elapsed_ns = start.elapsed().as_nanos() as u64;\n        hist.record(round_elapsed(elapsed_ns, inner)).unwrap();\n    }\n    let duration_s = run_start.elapsed().as_nanos() as f64 / 1e9;\n\n    (hist, duration_s)\n}"
}
```

{

  "old_string": "pub fn print_histogram(\n    name: &str,\n    iterations: u64,\n    inner: u64,\n    hist: &Histogram<u64>,\n    overhead: &Overhead,\n) {\n    let adj = overhead.per_call_ns(inner);\n    let total = iterations * inner;\n    println!(\n        \"{name} ({} iters × {} inner = {} calls; subtract {} ns/call)\",\n        fmt_commas(iterations),\n        inner,\n        fmt_commas(total),\n        fmt_commas_f64(adj, 2),\n    );",
  "new_string": "pub fn print_histogram(\n    name: &str,\n    iterations: u64,\n    inner: u64,\n    duration_s: f64,\n    hist: &Histogram<u64>,\n    overhead: &Overhead,\n) {\n    let adj = overhead.per_call_ns(inner);\n    let total = iterations * inner;\n    println!(\n        \"{name} [duration={:.1}s outer={} inner={} calls={} adj/call={}ns]\",\n        duration_s,\n        fmt_commas(iterations),\n        inner,\n        fmt_commas(total),\n        fmt_commas_f64(adj, 2),\n    );"
}

# iiac-perf

Measure performance of various Inter-Intra Application Communication
(IIAC) techniques.

## Design (0.2.0)

Design a Rust app that can measure the cost of various (IIAC) techniques.
By IIAC I mean all communication techniques between and within
applications such as regular function calls vs async function calls vs
channels vs serde/deser of json or zero-copy messaging using io_uring and
potentially any other technique. It would include between threads or
processes or apps on the same computer or between apps on the same LAN or
between apps over a WAN.

Ideally I'd like to be able to see a histogram of the range of times of the
send and receive costs, not just the mean/average of 10^3 or 10^6 invocations.
So the cost of the work should or could be something near-zero such as just
echoing the input, but maybe there is value in doing some constant but
variable amount of work to verify it's in-consequential. One thing I think we
need to be aware of is that if we make the work too simple the compiler could
just keep things in registers which would not be representative of "real" work.

We should start simple, like comparing normal and async function calls and
later expand to other techniques.

## Usage

```
iiac-perf [BENCH...] [-d SECONDS] [-i ITERATIONS] [-I INNER]
```

`BENCH` is one or more registered bench names, or `all` for every
registered bench. **With no arguments, `iiac-perf` prints the
available list and exits — that's the source of truth for which
benches the current build registers.**

Flags (also visible via `-h` / `--help`):
- `-d`, `--duration SECONDS` — target wall-clock seconds per bench
  (default `5.0`); iterations and INNER auto-size to hit this target.
  See chores `0.3.1-dev1` for the empirical study behind the default —
  longer (`-d 30`+) gives publication-grade stability. Mutually
  exclusive with `-D`.
- `-D`, `--total-duration SECONDS` — target total wall-clock seconds
  across all requested benches; budget is split equally per bench
  (e.g. `-D 30` with 6 benches → 5 s each). Mutually exclusive with
  `-d`.
- `-i`, `--iterations N` — override total iterations (INNER still
  adapts).
- `-I`, `--inner N` — override INNER (the inner-loop count per
  histogram sample). `INNER=1` measures single-call latency (each
  sample = one step). Higher INNER measures back-to-back / burst
  rate (each sample = N steps averaged, hides per-call jitter and
  parking costs).

Each bench prints a per-percentile histogram in nanoseconds with
both the raw measurement and an adjusted column (apparatus overhead
subtracted). Apparatus = `framing_per_sample / INNER + loop_per_iter`,
calibrated once at startup via a two-point fit on an empty bench.

Examples:

```
iiac-perf                        # list available benches
iiac-perf all                    # every bench, default ~5s each
iiac-perf min-now -d 30          # one bench, 30s budget
iiac-perf all -D 30              # ~30s total split equally
iiac-perf mpsc-2t -I 1           # explicit single-call latency
iiac-perf mpsc-2t -I 100         # back-to-back rate
```

## Workflow

Commits, pushes, and finalizes follow a per-step checkpoint flow
designed for this dual-repo (app + `.claude` bot session) setup.
See [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md#commit-push-finalize-flow) for the full
spec — single source of truth so the bot can't drift from the
human docs.

  "new_string": "3. `0.3.1` ✅ finalize: drop `-devN`, move todo entry to Done.\n\n## Add duration to bench header + logfmt-style metadata (0.3.2)\n\nUser asked for the bench header to show the wall-clock duration of\nthe measurement so it's easy to see how long a run actually took.\nWhile iterating on format we converged on logfmt-style bracketed\n`key=value` pairs — self-describing, easy to eyeball, trivial to\nparse.\n\n### Change\n\n- `run_bench` now times the whole sampling loop (not warmup/estimate)\n  and returns `(Histogram, duration_s)`.\n- `run_adaptive` threads `duration_s` through to callers; all four\n  bench `run()` functions pass it to `print_histogram`.\n- `print_histogram` header switches from\n  `{name} ({iters} iters × {inner} inner = {calls} calls; subtract {adj} ns/call)`\n  to\n  `{name} [duration={:.1}s outer={iters} inner={inner} calls={calls} adj/call={adj}ns]`.\n\n### Why logfmt\n\n- Brackets visually separate metadata from the name.\n- Space-separated `key=value` parses with any logfmt lib.\n- `outer`/`inner` pair more clearly than the old `iters`/`inner`.\n- `adj/call` is more descriptive than `subtract`.\n\nSingle-step bump 0.3.1 → 0.3.2 (mechanical change)."
}
```

> AGENT

Noted — saved that feedback. Apologies for the lost recap.

On the column alignment — yes, there's a better way. The current code hard-codes widths (`{:>10}`, `{:>11}`) picked by eyeballing what fits today. That breaks the moment a `max` exceeds a million ns with commas, or a `duration=12.3s` run pushes mean past 10 digits.

**Three options, cheapest to heaviest:**

- **A. Pre-compute column widths** (~20 lines): format every `raw` and `adj` value into `String`s first, take `max(len)` per column, then emit rows using that width. Self-adjusting, no crate, keeps current look. My preferred.
- **B. Use a table crate** like `tabled` or `comfy-table`: pretty, but overkill — you lose control of the "ns" suffix placement and it pulls in a dep.
- **C. Keep fixed widths, just widen them** to a "safely big" value (e.g. `{:>14}`): zero work, but leaves more whitespace than needed for common runs and can still overflow in pathological cases.

Want me to do A as a follow-up (0.3.3)?

adj).max(0.0);\n    let percentiles = [\n        (\"min\", hist.min()),\n        (\"p1\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.01)),\n        (\"p10\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.10)),\n        (\"p50\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.50)),\n        (\"p90\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.90)),\n        (\"p99\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.99)),\n        (\"p99.9\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.999)),\n        (\"p99.99\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.9999)),\n        (\"max\", hist.max()),\n    ];\n\n    let mut rows: Vec<(&str, String, Option<String>)> = percentiles\n        .iter()\n        .map(|(label, raw)| {\n            (\n                *label,\n                fmt_commas(*raw),\n                Some(fmt_commas_f64((*raw as f64 - adj).max(0.0), 1)),\n            )\n        })\n        .collect();\n    rows.push((\n        \"mean\",\n        fmt_commas_f64(mean, 1),\n        Some(fmt_commas_f64(adj_mean, 1)),\n    ));\n    rows.push((\"stdev\", fmt_commas_f64(hist.stdev(), 1), None));\n\n    let raw_w = rows.iter().map(|(_, r, _)| r.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);\n    let adj_w = rows\n        .iter()\n        .filter_map(|(_, _, a)| a.as_ref().map(String::len))\n        .max()\n        .unwrap_or(0);\n\n    const INDENT: &str = \"  \";\n    const LABEL_W: usize = 8;\n    const GAP: &str = \"    \";\n\n    let raw_col_end = INDENT.len() + LABEL_W + 1 + raw_w;\n    let adj_col_end = raw_col_end + \" ns\".len() + GAP.len() + adj_w;\n    println!(\n        \"{:>rw$}{:>aw$}\",\n        \"raw\",\n        \"adjusted\",\n        rw = raw_col_end,\n        aw = adj_col_end - raw_col_end,\n    );\n\n    for (label, raw_s, adj_s) in &rows {\n        match adj_s {\n            Some(a) => println!(\n                \"{INDENT}{label:<LABEL_W$} {raw_s:>raw_w$} ns{GAP}{a:>adj_w$} ns\"\n            ),\n            None => println!(\"{INDENT}{label:<LABEL_W$} {raw_s:>raw_w$} ns\"),\n        }\n    }\n    println!();\n}"
}

Fixed widths are fragile — they overflow on\nbig `max` values and leave excess whitespace on small runs.\n\n### Change\n\n`print_histogram` now:\n\n1. Formats every `raw` and `adj` value into a `String` up front.\n2. Takes `max(len)` per column to derive `raw_w` / `adj_w`.\n3. Emits the header row right-aligned to match the numeric column\n   right-edges (computed from indent + label + width + \" ns\" + gap).\n4. Emits each data row using the computed widths; `stdev` (no adj)\n   falls through a `match` arm that skips the adjusted column.\n\n`print_row` helper removed — rendering is now inline with the widths\nin scope.\n\n### Why\n\n- No more manual width tuning when values get bigger.\n- Each run's table is internally consistent regardless of scale\n  (sub-ns to multi-ms).\n- Adds ~15 lines of code but deletes the brittle hard-coded widths.\n\nSingle-step bump 0.3.2 → 0.3.3 (mechanical refactor, same visible\nlayout, widths now data-driven)."
}

  "new_string": "pub fn print_histogram(\n    name: &str,\n    iterations: u64,\n    inner: u64,\n    duration_s: f64,\n    hist: &Histogram<u64>,\n    overhead: &Overhead,\n) {\n    // Header line: bench name + logfmt-style metadata. `adj` is the\n    // apparatus overhead subtracted from each sample downstream.\n    let adj = overhead.per_call_ns(inner);\n    let total = iterations * inner;\n    println!(\n        \"{name} [duration={:.1}s outer={} inner={} calls={} adj/call={}ns]:\",\n        duration_s,\n        fmt_commas(iterations),\n        inner,\n        fmt_commas(total),\n        fmt_commas_f64(adj, 2),\n    );\n\n    // Collect the values we want to display. Percentiles + max all come\n    // from the histogram; mean and stdev are scalar stats.\n    let mean = hist.mean();\n    let adj_mean = (mean - adj).max(0.0);\n    let percentiles = [\n        (\"min\", hist.min()),\n        (\"p1\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.01)),\n        (\"p10\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.10)),\n        (\"p50\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.50)),\n        (\"p90\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.90)),\n        (\"p99\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.99)),\n        (\"p99.9\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.999)),\n        (\"p99.99\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.9999)),\n        (\"max\", hist.max()),\n    ];\n\n    // Format every value into its final String so we can size columns\n    // from the actual rendered widths. Each row is (label, raw, adj?);\n    // stdev has no adjusted column hence the Option.\n    let mut rows: Vec<(&str, String, Option<String>)> = percentiles\n        .iter()\n        .map(|(label, raw)| {\n            (\n                *label,\n                fmt_commas(*raw),\n                Some(fmt_commas_f64((*raw as f64 - adj).max(0.0), 1)),\n            )\n        })\n        .collect();\n    rows.push((\n        \"mean\",\n        fmt_commas_f64(mean, 1),\n        Some(fmt_commas_f64(adj_mean, 1)),\n    ));\n    rows.push((\"stdev\", fmt_commas_f64(hist.stdev(), 1), None));\n\n    // Column widths are data-driven: take the widest rendered string in\n    // each column so commas/decimals never overflow the field.\n    let raw_w = rows.iter().map(|(_, r, _)| r.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);\n    let adj_w = rows\n        .iter()\n        .filter_map(|(_, _, a)| a.as_ref().map(String::len))\n        .max()\n        .unwrap_or(0);\n\n    // Fixed layout pieces: 2-space indent, 8-wide label, 4-space gap\n    // between the raw and adjusted columns.\n    const INDENT: &str = \"  \";\n    const LABEL_W: usize = 8;\n    const GAP: &str = \"    \";\n\n    // \"raw\"/\"adjusted\" header sits over the numeric columns. We right-\n    // align each label to the right-edge of its column (just before the\n    // \" ns\" suffix), computed from indent + label + width + \" ns\" + gap.\n    let raw_col_end = INDENT.len() + LABEL_W + 1 + raw_w;\n    let adj_col_end = raw_col_end + \" ns\".len() + GAP.len() + adj_w;\n    println!(\n        \"{:>rw$}{:>aw$}\",\n        \"raw\",\n        \"adjusted\",\n        rw = raw_col_end,\n        aw = adj_col_end - raw_col_end,\n    );\n\n    // Data rows: label left-aligned, numbers right-aligned in their\n    // data-driven widths. stdev skips the adjusted column.\n    for (label, raw_s, adj_s) in &rows {\n        match adj_s {\n            Some(a) => println!(\"{INDENT}{label:<LABEL_W$} {raw_s:>raw_w$} ns{GAP}{a:>adj_w$} ns\"),\n            None => println!(\"{INDENT}{label:<LABEL_W$} {raw_s:>raw_w$} ns\"),\n        }\n    }\n    println!();\n}"
}
```

> DEVELOPER

Why can mean be int the "percentiles" array and that maybe it should be "values" or ...?

> AGENT

Good catch. `percentiles` is the wrong name anyway (`min`/`max` aren't percentiles), and the split between the array + two `push`es is arbitrary.

Two options:

**A. Just rename** the array to `stats` or `values`. Minimal change, keeps the u64/f64 split.

**B. Unify the rows** — use small helpers to produce `(label, raw_str, adj_str?)` directly so everything lives in one list:
```rust
fn row_u64(label, raw, adj) -> (&str, String, Option<String>) { ... }
fn row_f64(label, raw, adj) -> (&str, String, Option<String>) { ... }
fn row_raw_only(label, raw) -> (&str, String, Option<String>) { ... }

let rows = vec![
    row_u64("min",    hist.min(),                 adj),
    row_u64("p1",     hist.value_at_quantile(0.01), adj),
    ...
    row_u64("max",    hist.max(),                 adj),
    row_f64("mean",   hist.mean(),                adj),
    row_raw_only("stdev", hist.stdev()),
];
```
Then the whole "collect + format + push mean + push stdev" phase collapses to one list. No intermediate array needed.

I'd go B — it actually reduces the asymmetry rather than just masking it with a rename. Your call?

> DEVELOPER

agree

> DEVELOPER

We're iterating and jumping to the commit is premature; I tweaked raw|adj_col_end code.

> AGENT

Understood — I'll hold off on commits until you say ready.

On your tweak: the expression `raw_col_end + " ns".len() + GAP.len() + adj_w - raw_col_end` adds and subtracts `raw_col_end`, so it simplifies to `" ns".len() + GAP.len() + adj_w`. And the variable name `adj_col_end` no longer matches what it holds — it's now the gap (distance from where "raw" ends to where "adjusted" ends), not an absolute column-end position.

Two clean options:

**A.** Rename to reflect what it is:
```rust
let raw_col_end = INDENT.len() + LABEL_W + 1 + raw_w;
let adj_col_gap = " ns".len() + GAP.len() + adj_w;
println!("{:>raw_col_end$}{:>adj_col_gap$}", "raw", "adjusted");
```

**B.** Keep the two absolute positions (what I had before), which reads as "both columns end here, here":
```rust
let raw_col_end = INDENT.len() + LABEL_W + 1 + raw_w;
let adj_col_end = raw_col_end + " ns".len() + GAP.len() + adj_w;
let adj_col_gap = adj_col_end - raw_col_end;
println!("{:>raw_col_end$}{:>adj_col_gap$}", "raw", "adjusted");
```

A is tighter; B makes the derivation visible. I'd pick A — the subtraction round-trip in B is just documentation. Thoughts?

120	pub fn print_histogram(

152	        row_u64("p99.9", hist.value_at_quantile(0.999), adj),
153	        row_u64("p99.99", hist.value_at_quantile(0.9999), adj),
154	        row_u64("max", hist.max(), adj),

> DEVELOPER

Do you agree the comment is accurate?

> AGENT

Yes — the comment is accurate. Tracing a concrete layout confirms it:

With `raw_w=7`, `adj_w=9`, the data rows put the raw number ending at col 17 and the adj number ending at col 33 (both followed by ` ns`). The header's `{:>raw_col_end=18$}` right-aligns "raw" ending at col 17; the concatenated `{:>adj_col_end=16$}` right-aligns "adjusted" ending at col 33. Both labels land exactly at the right-edges of their numeric columns, just before the ` ns` suffix — which is what the comment says.

> DEVELOPER

I wonder if using a `struct Row { .. }` might be more obvious then the tuple?

> AGENT

Yes — with 3 fields the tuple already creates friction (`.0`/`.1`/`.2` at call sites, pattern destructures to track). A struct makes each use site self-describing:

```rust
struct Row {
    label: &'static str,
    raw: String,
    adj: Option<String>,
}

impl Row {
    fn from_u64(label: &'static str, raw: u64, adj: f64) -> Self {
        Self {
            label,
            raw: fmt_commas(raw),
            adj: Some(fmt_commas_f64((raw as f64 - adj).max(0.0), 1)),
        }
    }
    fn from_f64(label: &'static str, raw: f64, adj: f64) -> Self { ... }
    fn raw_only(label: &'static str, raw: f64) -> Self { ... }
}
```

Call sites become:
```rust
let rows = vec![
    Row::from_u64("min", hist.min(), adj),
    ...
    Row::from_f64("mean", hist.mean(), adj),
    Row::raw_only("stdev", hist.stdev()),
];

let raw_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.raw.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
let adj_w = rows.iter().filter_map(|r| r.adj.as_ref().map(String::len)).max().unwrap_or(0);

for Row { label, raw, adj } in &rows {
    match adj { ... }
}
```

Clearer without bloat. Go ahead?

> DEVELOPER

I wonder if both raw and adj should be f64, I think the mantisa is large enough for our likely loop ranges?

> AGENT

Precision-wise, yes — f64 mantissa is 53 bits (~9×10¹⁵), way more than we need (we're dealing with ns, 60s bench = 6×10¹⁰ ns max). Safe.

**But** there's a display side-effect worth deciding first. Histogram percentiles/min/max are recorded as integer ns, so today they print without decimals (`310 ns`). Mean/stdev are `f64` and print with 1 decimal (`7,129.1 ns`). If we unify to `f64` and pick one format for all, we get either:

- **Uniform `1 decimal`**: percentiles print as `310.0 ns` — consistent but a little noisy for whole-ns values.
- **Uniform `0 decimals`**: mean/stdev lose their fractional part (`7,129 ns`) — drops sub-ns resolution.
- **Keep two formats**: `Row::new` (1 decimal for float stats) + `Row::from_u64` (0 decimals for integer stats). Two constructors remain but the u64/f64 choice is now purely display-driven, not precision-driven.

If you want a single constructor the right answer is probably uniform 1 decimal — we already show sub-ns detail in the `adjusted` column anyway, so `310.0 ns` raw next to `298.4 ns` adj reads naturally.

Which way?

> DEVELOPER

Can't we specify number of decimal digits when we convert them to strings?

> AGENT

Yes — `fmt_commas_f64(value, decimals)` already takes a decimals count, so f64-in-struct + format-at-print-time works. One nuance though: today the *raw* and *adj* columns use **different** decimals:

- percentile raw: `0` (integer ns) → `310`
- percentile adj: `1` (overhead subtraction produces fractional) → `298.4`
- mean raw / adj: `1` → `7,129.1`
- stdev raw: `1` → `1,439.5`

So raw's decimals vary per row, adj's is always `1`. If we go f64-in-struct, we need to carry that per-row hint:

```rust
struct Row {
    label: &'static str,
    raw: f64,
    raw_decimals: usize,
    adj: Option<f64>,   // rendered with 1 decimal, always
}

impl Row {
    fn integer(label: &'static str, raw: u64, overhead: f64) -> Self {
        let r = raw as f64;
        Self { label, raw: r, raw_decimals: 0, adj: Some((r - overhead).max(0.0)) }
    }
    fn float(label: &'static str, raw: f64, overhead: f64) -> Self {
        Self { label, raw, raw_decimals: 1, adj: Some((raw - overhead).max(0.0)) }
    }
    fn raw_only(label: &'static str, raw: f64) -> Self {
        Self { label, raw, raw_decimals: 1, adj: None }
    }
}
```

Column widths still need rendered strings, so we'd format each row to `(raw_s, adj_s)` before the width pass — effectively the same formatting happens either way. Compared to storing `String` directly in the struct, the f64-version adds a `raw_decimals` field but keeps the numeric values around. Mild win on "data is data, display is display" grounds; no real functional difference.

Want me to go f64-in-struct?

> DEVELOPER

Maybe, but do we actually have another digit of precision or should we just print both without the decimal fractional part?

> AGENT

Good question — let's check actual precision:

- **Raw percentiles / min / max**: histogram stores `u64` ns. Single-sample timer resolution is ~1ns (minstant TSC). So raw has genuine 1ns precision, nothing below.
- **Raw mean**: `sum / count` over many samples → averaging recovers genuine sub-ns structure (noise → 0 as 1/√N). So mean *does* have legitimate sub-ns precision.
- **Raw stdev**: spread of the population, also legitimately sub-ns.
- **Adj on percentiles**: `raw - overhead` where raw is integer ns and overhead is f64 (e.g. `11.60`). The `.4` you see in `298.4 ns` is arithmetic from the overhead constant, **not** measurement precision. It's sub-ns theater.
- **Adj on mean/stdev**: same subtraction, but the raw itself has real sub-ns info, so the fractional stays meaningful.

**So**: drop decimals for percentile rows (both raw and adj), keep 1 decimal for mean/stdev. That collapses the per-column question — decimals becomes a single Row property:

```rust
struct Row {
    label: &'static str,
    raw: f64,
    adj: Option<f64>,
    decimals: usize,   // 0 for integer-valued stats, 1 for averaged stats
}

impl Row {
    fn integer(label: &'static str, raw: u64, overhead: f64) -> Self {
        let r = raw as f64;
        Self { label, raw: r, adj: Some((r - overhead).max(0.0)), decimals: 0 }
    }
    fn float(label: &'static str, raw: f64, overhead: f64) -> Self {
        Self { label, raw, adj: Some((raw - overhead).max(0.0)), decimals: 1 }
    }
    fn raw_only(label: &'static str, raw: f64) -> Self {
        Self { label, raw, adj: None, decimals: 1 }
    }
}
```

Bonus: the displayed numbers become honest about what's real vs computed. Want me to implement?

> DEVELOPER

y

> DEVELOPER

It looks odd, having mean and stdev having a 100ps presision and I don't think it adds that much information, rounding to whole ns would seem to be more honest, and we can't even calculate the duration very well, we asked for 60 and got 46.8 and 35.7!

> AGENT

Agreed — 100ps precision is a false promise. With ~1ns timer res and ~25% run-to-run duration variance, the `.1` is numerical seasoning.

Also lets me simplify the struct further (no `decimals` field needed) and drop one constructor:

```rust
struct Row {
    label: &'static str,
    raw: f64,
    adj: Option<f64>,
}

impl Row {
    fn with_adj(label: &'static str, raw: f64, overhead: f64) -> Self {
        Self { label, raw, adj: Some((raw - overhead).max(0.0)) }
    }
    fn raw_only(label: &'static str, raw: f64) -> Self {
        Self { label, raw, adj: None }
    }
}
```

Call sites all pass f64 (histogram u64 methods get a single `as f64` cast):
```rust
Row::with_adj("min",    hist.min() as f64,                    adj),
Row::with_adj("p1",     hist.value_at_quantile(0.01) as f64,  adj),
...
Row::with_adj("mean",   hist.mean(),                          adj),
Row::raw_only("stdev",  hist.stdev()),
```

Implementing now. The duration miss (60→46.8 / 35.7 = 22%–40% undershoot) is a separate adaptive-sizing issue worth a follow-up — estimator runs on cold samples and the picked iteration count underestimates when the real loop is faster than the startup probe. I'll flag as a followup once this lands.

  "new_string": "    // All stats displayed as whole ns — timer resolution is ~1 ns and\n    // cross-run duration variance dwarfs sub-ns structure, so extra\n    // decimals would overclaim precision. stdev has no adjusted column\n    // since subtracting overhead from a spread is meaningless.\n    let rows: Vec<Row> = vec![\n        Row::with_adj(\"min\", hist.min() as f64, adj),\n        Row::with_adj(\"p1\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.01) as f64, adj),\n        Row::with_adj(\"p10\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.10) as f64, adj),\n        Row::with_adj(\"p50\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.50) as f64, adj),\n        Row::with_adj(\"p90\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.90) as f64, adj),\n        Row::with_adj(\"p99\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.99) as f64, adj),\n        Row::with_adj(\"p99.9\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.999) as f64, adj),\n        Row::with_adj(\"p99.99\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.9999) as f64, adj),\n        Row::with_adj(\"max\", hist.max() as f64, adj),\n        Row::with_adj(\"mean\", hist.mean(), adj),\n        Row::raw_only(\"stdev\", hist.stdev()),\n    ];\n\n    // Render each row to its final strings so we can size columns from\n    // the actual widths.\n    let rendered: Vec<(&str, String, Option<String>)> = rows\n        .iter()\n        .map(|r| {\n            (\n                r.label,\n                fmt_commas_f64(r.raw, 0),\n                r.adj.map(|a| fmt_commas_f64(a, 0)),\n            )\n        })\n        .collect();"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "struct Row {\n    label: &'static str,\n    raw: f64,\n    adj: Option<f64>,\n    decimals: usize,\n}\n\nimpl Row {\n    /// Integer-valued stat (percentile / min / max): 0 decimals. The adj\n    /// column is still computed so overhead subtraction is visible, but\n    /// also rendered with 0 decimals since single-sample precision is 1 ns.\n    fn integer(label: &'static str, raw: u64, overhead: f64) -> Self {\n        let r = raw as f64;\n        Self {\n            label,\n            raw: r,\n            adj: Some((r - overhead).max(0.0)),\n            decimals: 0,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Averaged stat (mean): 1 decimal. Averaging over many samples recovers\n    /// genuine sub-ns structure.\n    fn float(label: &'static str, raw: f64, overhead: f64) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            label,\n            raw,\n            adj: Some((raw - overhead).max(0.0)),\n            decimals: 1,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Spread stat (stdev): 1 decimal, no adjusted column — overhead\n    /// subtraction on a spread measure is meaningless.\n    fn raw_only(label: &'static str, raw: f64) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            label,\n            raw,\n            adj: None,\n            decimals: 1,\n        }\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "struct Row {\n    label: &'static str,\n    raw: f64,\n    adj: Option<f64>,\n}\n\nimpl Row {\n    fn with_adj(label: &'static str, raw: f64, overhead: f64) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            label,\n            raw,\n            adj: Some((raw - overhead).max(0.0)),\n        }\n    }\n\n    fn raw_only(label: &'static str, raw: f64) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            label,\n            raw,\n            adj: None,\n        }\n    }\n}"
}

On the duration miss (60s → 46.8 / 35.7 — 22% and 40% undershoot): the root cause is that `pick_iterations` extrapolates from a cold startup probe (5×1000 steps before warmup settles). If the cold probe runs slower than steady-state (thread spinup, cold caches, MPSC queue not yet in groove), we underestimate capacity and run short.

Two fix options as a follow-up (probably 0.3.4):

- **A. Time-based loop** (~10 lines): drop `pick_iterations` for the default path; loop `run_bench` until `start.elapsed() >= target`. Hit duration exactly. `-i` override still takes an explicit count for reproducibility. This is what criterion/hyperfine-style harnesses do.
- **B. Re-estimate mid-run**: run a fraction of predicted iterations, measure actual rate, extend remaining iterations accordingly. More complex, same robustness as A.

I'd do **A** — simpler, more honest, the output already reports actual duration so there's no deception.

Commit 0.3.3 (column auto-size + struct + whole-ns + Calibration:) first, then tackle duration in 0.3.4 — OK?

> DEVELOPER

So do you agree, we don't have 100ps precision and with the "max" differing by almost 3x it doesn't seem reasonable at all. My computer is doing other stuff so this is more noisy that probably typical, but that isn't unusual.

> AGENT

Fully agree. Between the two runs you showed:
- `max` varied **2.8×** (9.2M → 3.3M ns) — single stray outliers
- `p99.99` varied **1.4×** (17.5k → 23.9k ns) — tail noise
- `mean` varied **5%** (5,444 → 5,192 ns)
- `duration` varied **31%** (46.8 → 35.7 s)

With that much run-to-run spread from external load, scheduler jitter, and thermal/TSC drift, sub-ns on any column is fiction. Even 1-ns-precision display is already optimistic for the tail — the real signal lives in broad shape (p50 stable, long right tail from OS interruptions). Whole-ns everywhere is the honest choice.

And yeah, a loaded desktop just makes it louder — doesn't change the underlying physics. A quiet isolated CPU would tighten the percentiles but max/tail would still be governed by kernel events you can't avoid.

Ready when you are to commit 0.3.3 and then tackle duration calibration as 0.3.4.

> DEVELOPER

IIRC, there is something else we can do we can assign a thread to a CPU which can help reduce some of the noise?

> AGENT

Yes — CPU affinity (pinning threads to specific cores) is the big one. It stops the scheduler from migrating the bench thread mid-measurement, which otherwise shows up as cache-cold spikes in your p99.9 / max. On a 3900X (4 CCXs × 3 cores in Zen 2, cross-CCX L3 hop ≈ 2–3× intra-CCX), pinning also lets you pick core pairs that share L3 for mpsc-2t, keeping the channel hot.

**Three layers, increasingly invasive:**

1. **External, no code change**: `taskset -c 2,3 iiac-perf mpsc-2t -d 60` — pins the whole process to cores 2–3. Zero work, fine for quick experiments.
2. **In-process**: add `core_affinity` crate, pin the main thread before the bench loop; for mpsc-2t also pin the worker thread to a sibling core. Optional `--pin <cores>` CLI flag, default unpinned to avoid surprising users.
3. **Full system quieting**: `isolcpus=` kernel param, `cpufreq` governor = performance, disable turbo, SCHED_FIFO priority. Out-of-scope for this repo, but worth a README note.

**Related but often bigger wins:**
- Frequency governor: run `sudo cpupower frequency-set -g performance` before benching. Cuts mean drift from boost transitions.
- Pinning alone helps tail latency more than mean; if you want tighter means, governor matters more.

My take: **do layer 2 as 0.3.5** — it's small (~30 lines, one crate), and 0.3.4 is still the duration fix we agreed on. Or we can do pinning first if you'd rather see the noise drop before worrying about duration.

Which first — duration (0.3.4) or pinning (0.3.4, deferring duration)?

> AGENT

Yep — honest, but the 80 ns – 2.4 ms spread is mixing real measurement with system noise, and both ends are suspect:

**Min = 80 ns** isn't nonsense though — it's physically achievable for a cache-hot MPSC when the worker is already spinning on `req_rx.recv()`: `send` + cross-core cache-line transfer (~50 ns on Zen 2) + `recv` of an already-produced response. That's the **best-case fast path** the system can deliver. Not BS — just an atypical timing alignment. The problem isn't accuracy, it's representativeness.

**Max = 2.4 ms** is almost certainly a scheduler preemption — the kernel descheduled our thread for ~2ms while the timer was running. Nothing about the MPSC round-trip itself; pure OS noise.

**Real MPSC round-trip cost is probably ~6.5μs (p50)** — the rest is tail behavior of the environment.

Things we could do to stop mixing signal with environment:

- **CPU pinning** (the 0.3.5 we already talked about) — biggest lever. Stops migration-driven TSC reads and cache-cold spikes. Tail will shrink dramatically (not the 2.4ms, that's still kernel preemption, but the p99–p99.99 band should tighten).
- **De-emphasize min/max**: swap `min` → `p0.1`, drop `max` or move it to a separate "extremes" section with a note that it's dominated by scheduler events. Percentile-based bounds are more stable and what criterion/perf tools usually report.
- **Warmup-window discard**: drop first N samples (first ~1s worth) to ignore cold caches / TSC settling. Currently we do 10k warmup but the histogram starts fresh right after — first real samples can still be migration-prone.
- **README note**: explain that min/max bound measurement range, not operation cost, and point readers to p50/p99 for actionable numbers.

None of this is urgent. I'd do them in this order: **pinning** (0.3.4 or 0.3.5) → **duration fix** → **min/max handling** (could be the README note alone, or a flag to hide them).

Want to pivot 0.3.4 to pinning since it's the highest-impact lever, and push duration to 0.3.5?

> AGENT

Before diving in, quick plan for **0.3.4 — CPU pinning**:

**Scope:**
- Add `core_affinity` dep (~8KB, simple cross-platform wrapper)
- New CLI flag `--pin <CORE-LIST>` (e.g. `--pin 0,1`). First core → main/measurement thread; subsequent cores → worker threads. Omitted = no pinning (unchanged default).
- Thread `pin_cores` through `RunCfg` so `mpsc_2t` can pin its worker on spawn.
- Apply main-thread pinning in `main()` before calling each bench's `run(cfg)`.
- README note explaining the flag + recommending same-CCX core pairs on Zen (e.g. `0,1` or `3,4` for 3900X).

**Versioning**: single-step 0.3.3 → 0.3.4. One focused feature, small blast radius.

**Behavior verification**: run `mpsc-2t -d 10 --pin 0,1` a few times and compare p99/p99.9/p99.99 to unpinned runs to confirm tail shrinks.

OK to proceed?

> DEVELOPER

hmm, I'm planing on doing tests with potentally many threads let's I could envision tests with N threads per vCPU?

> AGENT

Good — let me widen the design so it doesn't need a rewrite when you add N-thread benches.

**Proposal:**

```
--pin <CORES>
```

`CORES` is a comma-separated list with range shorthand: `0,1` / `0-11` / `0,3-5,7` / `0,0` (oversubscribe same core). Parsed once into `Vec<usize>` ≡ the **core pool**.

Threads get cores **positionally with wrap-around**:
- Bench asks for thread `i`, gets `cores[i % cores.len()]`.
- Wrap enables oversubscription naturally: `--pin 0,1` on a 4-thread bench → cores `0,1,0,1`; `--pin 0-11` on 24 threads → `0,1,…,11,0,1,…,11`.
- Empty pool (no `--pin` flag) = no pinning, unchanged default.

**Bench contract:**
- Each bench knows how many threads it spawns. `mpsc-2t` uses cores[0] for main and cores[1] for worker (with wrap, so `--pin 0,0` co-locates them).
- A small helper `cfg.core_for(thread_idx) -> Option<usize>` returns `Some(id)` if pool non-empty, else `None`. Benches call `pin_current_thread(core_for(i))` and it no-ops when `None`.

**What this lets you do later without touching the CLI:**
- N-worker fanout bench: just uses cores[0..N].
- Oversubscription: `--pin 0,0,0,0` packs 4 threads on core 0 — demonstrates contention.
- Pool bigger than threads: extra cores ignored; useful for defining an "allowed" set.

**What I'm deferring:**
- NUMA / CCX awareness (auto-pick "same L3" pairs). Topology detection is OS-specific; if you want it, call it out as 0.3.5+.
- `--threads-per-core` multiplier. Redundant with wrap-around (just repeat cores in the list).

**Scope for 0.3.4:**
- `--pin` parser (list + ranges)
- `RunCfg::core_for()` helper
- `pin_current_thread(Option<usize>)` helper in a small `pin` module
- Wire into existing benches (mpsc_2t pins main + worker; single-thread benches pin main only)

Single-step 0.3.3 → 0.3.4. OK?

> DEVELOPER

> AGENT

Good question — I was sloppy. Three distinct concepts, usually conflated:

- **Physical core** (inxi's "12-core"): the actual silicon unit. 3900X has 12.
- **Logical CPU** / **hardware thread** / **vCPU** (System Monitor's 24): an OS-visible execution context. Each physical core on 3900X runs 2 SMT threads = 24 logical.
- **CCX/CCD**: Zen grouping — 3 physical cores sharing 16MB L3 per CCX on Zen 2, 4 CCXs total.

**Kernel affinity APIs (and `core_affinity`, `taskset`) operate on *logical CPUs*** — IDs 0–23 on your box. So when I wrote "--pin 0,1" earlier, I meant logical CPUs 0 and 1. Whether those are:
- two SMT siblings of the same physical core (contending for L1/L2/exec units — terrible for benchmarking), or
- two separate physical cores in the same CCX (good: share L3, independent execution), or
- two separate physical cores in different CCXs (bad: cross-CCX L3 ≈ 2–3× latency)

…depends on your kernel's enumeration. Typical Linux on Zen 2 puts logical N and N+12 as SMT siblings, so `0` and `12` are the same physical core, `0` and `1` are different physical cores (often same CCX). But this **varies** — check `cat /sys/devices/system/cpu/cpu0/topology/thread_siblings_list` to be sure.

**Revised terminology for the plan:** I'll call them **logical CPUs** (what the API takes). Docs should note the SMT sibling thing with a `--pin 0,12` "both on same physical core (intentional contention)" vs `--pin 0,1` "different physical cores" example.

**Does this change the design?** No — the `--pin <list>` model still works; we're just clearer about what the numbers mean. Want me to proceed on that basis?

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/pin.rs",
  "content": "use std::collections::BTreeSet;\n\n/// Parse a `--pin` value (comma-separated list with optional ranges) into\n/// an ordered vector of logical CPU IDs. Accepts forms like `\"0,1\"`,\n/// `\"0-11\"`, `\"0,3-5,7\"`. Duplicates are preserved in the given order so\n/// oversubscription (`\"0,0,0\"`) works.\npub fn parse_cores(spec: &str) -> Result<Vec<usize>, String> {\n    let mut out = Vec::new();\n    for part in spec.split(',').map(str::trim).filter(|s| !s.is_empty()) {\n        match part.split_once('-') {\n            None => out.push(\n                part.parse::<usize>()\n                    .map_err(|e| format!(\"invalid core id {part:?}: {e}\"))?,\n            ),\n            Some((lo, hi)) => {\n                let lo = lo\n                    .trim()\n                    .parse::<usize>()\n                    .map_err(|e| format!(\"invalid range start {lo:?}: {e}\"))?;\n                let hi = hi\n                    .trim()\n                    .parse::<usize>()\n                    .map_err(|e| format!(\"invalid range end {hi:?}: {e}\"))?;\n                if hi < lo {\n                    return Err(format!(\"range {lo}-{hi} is empty\"));\n                }\n                out.extend(lo..=hi);\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    Ok(out)\n}\n\n/// Pin the current thread to `logical_cpu`. No-op if `None` or if the CPU\n/// id isn't present on this machine.\npub fn pin_current(logical_cpu: Option<usize>) {\n    let Some(target) = logical_cpu else { return };\n    let Some(ids) = core_affinity::get_core_ids() else {\n        return;\n    };\n    if let Some(id) = ids.into_iter().find(|c| c.id == target) {\n        core_affinity::set_for_current(id);\n    }\n}\n\n/// Report the pinning plan (what the user asked for) as a human-readable\n/// summary for the startup banner.\npub fn plan_summary(cores: &[usize]) -> String {\n    if cores.is_empty() {\n        return \"none (unpinned)\".to_string();\n    }\n    let unique: BTreeSet<usize> = cores.iter().copied().collect();\n    format!(\n        \"{cores:?} ({} slot{}, {} unique CPU{})\",\n        cores.len(),\n        if cores.len() == 1 { \"\" } else { \"s\" },\n        unique.len(),\n        if unique.len() == 1 { \"\" } else { \"s\" },\n    )\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_plain_list() {\n        assert_eq!(parse_cores(\"0,1,2\").unwrap(), vec![0, 1, 2]);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_range() {\n        assert_eq!(parse_cores(\"0-3\").unwrap(), vec![0, 1, 2, 3]);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_mixed() {\n        assert_eq!(parse_cores(\"0,3-5,7\").unwrap(), vec![0, 3, 4, 5, 7]);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_duplicates_preserved() {\n        assert_eq!(parse_cores(\"0,0,0\").unwrap(), vec![0, 0, 0]);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_empty_string_ok() {\n        assert_eq!(parse_cores(\"\").unwrap(), Vec::<usize>::new());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_reverse_range_errs() {\n        assert!(parse_cores(\"5-3\").is_err());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_garbage_errs() {\n        assert!(parse_cores(\"abc\").is_err());\n        assert!(parse_cores(\"1-x\").is_err());\n    }\n}\n"
}

5	use clap::Parser;
6	
7	#[derive(Parser)]
8	#[command(version, about = "IIAC performance measurement", max_term_width = 80)]
9	struct Cli {
10	    /// Benches to run. Pass 'all' for every registered bench, or
11	    /// one or more names. Run with no args to see the available list.
12	    benches: Vec<String>,
13	
14	    /// Target wall-clock seconds per bench (default 5.0). Auto-sizes
15	    /// iterations and INNER. Mutually exclusive with -D.
16	    #[arg(short = 'd', long, conflicts_with = "total_duration")]
17	    duration: Option<f64>,
18	
19	    /// Target total wall-clock seconds across all requested benches;
20	    /// budget is split equally per bench. Mutually exclusive with -d.
21	    #[arg(short = 'D', long)]
22	    total_duration: Option<f64>,
23	
24	    /// Override iterations (skips auto-sizing of total count; INNER still adapts).
25	    #[arg(short, long)]
26	    iterations: Option<u64>,
27	
28	    /// Override INNER (skips auto-sizing of inner-loop count).
29	    /// INNER=1 measures single-call latency (each sample = one step); higher
30	    /// INNER measures back-to-back/burst rate (each sample = N steps averaged).
31	    #[arg(short = 'I', long)]
32	    inner: Option<u64>,
33	}
34	
35	const DEFAULT_DURATION: f64 = 5.0;
36	
37	fn main() {
38	    let cli = Cli::parse();
39	
40	    if cli.benches.is_empty() {
41	        println!("no benches specified. use -h or --help for more info.");
42	        println!("available: all, {}", benches::names().join(", "));
43	        return;
44	    }
45	
46	    println!(
47	        "iiac-perf {} — IIAC performance measurement\n",
48	        env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION")
49	    );
50	
51	    let overhead = overhead::calibrate();
52	    println!("Calibration:");
53	    println!(
54	        "  framing/sample    {:>7} ns  (timer pair, two-point fit)",
55	        harness::fmt_commas_f64(overhead.framing_per_sample_ns, 2)
56	    );
57	    println!(
58	        "  loop/iter         {:>7} ns  (per inner-loop iteration)",
59	        harness::fmt_commas_f64(overhead.loop_per_iter_ns, 2)
60	    );
61	    println!();
62	
63	    let runners = match benches::resolve(&cli.benches) {
64	        Ok(r) => r,
65	        Err(e) => {
66	            eprintln!("error: {e}");
67	            std::process::exit(2);
68	        }
69	    };
70	
71	    let target_seconds = match (cli.duration, cli.total_duration) {
72	        (Some(d), _) => d,
73	        (None, Some(t)) => t / runners.len() as f64,
74	        (None, None) => DEFAULT_DURATION,
75	    };
76	
77	    let cfg = harness::RunCfg {
78	        overhead: &overhead,
79	        target_seconds,
80	        iterations_override: cli.iterations,
81	        inner_override: cli.inner,
82	    };
83	
84	    for run in runners {
85	        run(&cfg);
86	    }
87	}
88

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/harness.rs",
  "old_string": "pub struct RunCfg<'a> {\n    pub overhead: &'a Overhead,\n    pub target_seconds: f64,\n    pub iterations_override: Option<u64>,\n    pub inner_override: Option<u64>,\n}",
  "new_string": "pub struct RunCfg<'a> {\n    pub overhead: &'a Overhead,\n    pub target_seconds: f64,\n    pub iterations_override: Option<u64>,\n    pub inner_override: Option<u64>,\n    /// Core pool for thread pinning. Indexed positionally with wrap-around\n    /// via `core_for(thread_idx)`; empty means no pinning.\n    pub pin_cores: &'a [usize],\n}\n\nimpl RunCfg<'_> {\n    /// CPU id for the bench's `thread_idx`-th thread, using wrap-around\n    /// over the pool. Returns `None` when the pool is empty so callers\n    /// can treat unpinned and pinned runs uniformly.\n    pub fn core_for(&self, thread_idx: usize) -> Option<usize> {\n        if self.pin_cores.is_empty() {\n            None\n        } else {\n            Some(self.pin_cores[thread_idx % self.pin_cores.len()])\n        }\n    }\n}"
}

  "old_string": "impl StdMpsc2Thread {\n    pub fn new() -> Self {\n        let (req_tx, req_rx) = mpsc::channel::<u64>();\n        let (resp_tx, resp_rx) = mpsc::channel::<u64>();\n        let worker = thread::spawn(move || {\n            while let Ok(v) = req_rx.recv() {\n                if resp_tx.send(v).is_err() {\n                    break;\n                }\n            }\n        });\n        Self {\n            req_tx,\n            resp_rx,\n            worker: Some(worker),\n            counter: 0,\n        }\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "impl StdMpsc2Thread {\n    pub fn new(worker_cpu: Option<usize>) -> Self {\n        let (req_tx, req_rx) = mpsc::channel::<u64>();\n        let (resp_tx, resp_rx) = mpsc::channel::<u64>();\n        let worker = thread::spawn(move || {\n            pin::pin_current(worker_cpu);\n            while let Ok(v) = req_rx.recv() {\n                if resp_tx.send(v).is_err() {\n                    break;\n                }\n            }\n        });\n        Self {\n            req_tx,\n            resp_rx,\n            worker: Some(worker),\n            counter: 0,\n        }\n    }\n}"
}

```
35	available list and exits — that's the source of truth for which
36	benches the current build registers.**
37	
38	Flags (also visible via `-h` / `--help`):
39	- `-d`, `--duration SECONDS` — target wall-clock seconds per bench
40	  (default `5.0`); iterations and INNER auto-size to hit this target.
41	  See chores `0.3.1-dev1` for the empirical study behind the default —
42	  longer (`-d 30`+) gives publication-grade stability. Mutually
43	  exclusive with `-D`.
44	- `-D`, `--total-duration SECONDS` — target total wall-clock seconds
45	  across all requested benches; budget is split equally per bench
46	  (e.g. `-D 30` with 6 benches → 5 s each). Mutually exclusive with
47	  `-d`.
48	- `-i`, `--iterations N` — override total iterations (INNER still
49	  adapts).
50	- `-I`, `--inner N` — override INNER (the inner-loop count per
51	  histogram sample). `INNER=1` measures single-call latency (each
52	  sample = one step). Higher INNER measures back-to-back / burst
53	  rate (each sample = N steps averaged, hides per-call jitter and
54	  parking costs).
55	
56	Each bench prints a per-percentile histogram in nanoseconds with
57	both the raw measurement and an adjusted column (apparatus overhead
58	subtracted). Apparatus = `framing_per_sample / INNER + loop_per_iter`,
59	calibrated once at startup via a two-point fit on an empty bench.
60	
61	Examples:
62	
63	```
64	iiac-perf                        # list available benches
65	iiac-perf all                    # every bench, default ~5s each
66	iiac-perf min-now -d 30          # one bench, 30s budget
67	iiac-perf all -D 30              # ~30s total split equally
68	iiac-perf mpsc-2t -I 1           # explicit single-call latency
69	iiac-perf mpsc-2t -I 100         # back-to-back rate
70	```
71	
72	## Workflow
73	
74	Commits, pushes, and finalizes follow a per-step checkpoint flow
75	designed for this dual-repo (app + `.claude` bot session) setup.
76	See [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md#commit-push-finalize-flow) for the full
77	spec — single source of truth so the bot can't drift from the
78	human docs.
79	
```

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/README.md",
  "old_string": "- `-I`, `--inner N` — override INNER (the inner-loop count per\n  histogram sample). `INNER=1` measures single-call latency (each\n  sample = one step). Higher INNER measures back-to-back / burst\n  rate (each sample = N steps averaged, hides per-call jitter and\n  parking costs).",
  "new_string": "- `-I`, `--inner N` — override INNER (the inner-loop count per\n  histogram sample). `INNER=1` measures single-call latency (each\n  sample = one step). Higher INNER measures back-to-back / burst\n  rate (each sample = N steps averaged, hides per-call jitter and\n  parking costs).\n- `--pin CORES` — pin bench threads to logical CPUs. `CORES` is a\n  comma-separated list with optional ranges: `0,1`, `0-5`, `0,3-5,7`.\n  Treated as a **core pool** indexed positionally with wrap-around, so\n  thread `i` gets `pool[i % pool.len()]`. Examples:\n  - `--pin 0,1` pins a 2-thread bench to logical CPUs 0 and 1.\n  - `--pin 0,0` co-locates two threads on the same CPU\n    (oversubscription — measures contention).\n  - `--pin 0-11` defines a 12-CPU pool for larger fanout benches;\n    threads wrap over it.\n\n  On AMD Zen 2 (e.g. Ryzen 9 3900X, 12 physical × 2 SMT = 24 logical\n  CPUs), logical IDs `N` and `N+12` are SMT siblings of the same\n  physical core. `--pin 0,12` pairs siblings (max resource contention);\n  `--pin 0,1` uses independent physical cores in the same CCX (best\n  latency for channel benches — shared L3, no SMT contention). Check\n  your topology with\n  `cat /sys/devices/system/cpu/cpu0/topology/thread_siblings_list`.\n\n  Typical measured effect on `mpsc-2t` at `-d 10` (3900X, idle desktop):\n  unpinned mean ≈ 7,044 ns / stdev ≈ 6,545 ns / p99.99 ≈ 74 µs;\n  `--pin 0,1` → mean ≈ 5,636 ns / stdev ≈ 1,321 ns / p99.99 ≈ 17 µs.\n  Tail tightens ~4×, stdev ~5×, mean drops ~20 %."
}
```

id: toolu_01GqdKjL83Qjo8TmYBe824PF
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/notes/chores-01.md",
  "old_string": "Single-step bump 0.3.2 → 0.3.3 (mechanical refactor, same visible\nlayout, widths now data-driven).",
  "new_string": "Single-step bump 0.3.2 → 0.3.3 (mechanical refactor, same visible\nlayout, widths now data-driven).\n\n## Add `--pin` CPU affinity flag (0.3.4)\n\nObserved that unpinned `mpsc-2t` produces an 80 ns min alongside a\n3.7 ms max — honest but mixes real measurement with scheduler\nnoise. Thread migration also invalidates TSC reads across CPUs\n(minstant `start`/`elapsed` may span cores if the kernel moves us\nmid-measurement), so pinning helps accuracy *and* reproducibility.\n\n### Change\n\n- New dep: `core_affinity = \"0.8\"`.\n- New module: `src/pin.rs` with `parse_cores`, `pin_current`, and\n  `plan_summary`. Parser accepts commas + ranges + duplicates (e.g.\n  `0,1`, `0-5`, `0,3-5,7`, `0,0`).\n- `--pin CORES` CLI flag (comma-separated list / ranges). Treated\n  as a **core pool**: thread `i` pins to `pool[i % pool.len()]`.\n  Wrap-around handles oversubscription naturally.\n- `RunCfg::core_for(thread_idx) -> Option<usize>` helper returns\n  the pinning target (or `None` when the pool is empty so callers\n  treat unpinned and pinned uniformly).\n- `main()` pins the measurement thread to `pool[0]` before\n  calibration so framing overhead is measured on the same CPU used\n  for benches.\n- `mpsc_2t::StdMpsc2Thread::new` takes `worker_cpu: Option<usize>`\n  and pins the worker on spawn.\n- Single-thread benches (`min_now`, `std_now`, `mpsc_1t`) inherit\n  pinning automatically since they run on the (already-pinned)\n  main thread.\n- Banner adds `pinning  [0, 1] (2 slots, 2 unique CPUs)` line.\n\n### Why logical-CPU terminology\n\n`core_affinity`, `taskset`, and the Linux scheduler all operate on\n*logical* CPU IDs — i.e. SMT threads. The docs previously conflated\n\"core\" (physical silicon) with \"logical CPU\" (OS-visible execution\ncontext). On 3900X: 12 physical cores × 2 SMT = 24 logical CPUs.\nLogical `N` and `N+12` are SMT siblings of the same physical core\n(confirmed via `/sys/devices/system/cpu/cpu0/topology/thread_siblings_list`\n→ `0,12`). README explains the distinction with `--pin 0,1`\n(independent cores, same CCX) vs `--pin 0,12` (SMT siblings).\n\n### Measured effect (3900X, idle desktop, `-d 10`)\n\n| pinning | mean | stdev | p99.99 | max |\n|---------|-----:|------:|-------:|----:|\n| none | 7,044 ns | 6,545 ns | 74 µs | 3.7 ms |\n| `0,1` (independent) | 5,636 ns | 1,321 ns | 17 µs | 311 µs |\n| `0,12` (SMT siblings) | 5,744 ns | 1,476 ns | 16 µs | 66 µs |\n\nStdev dropped ~5×, p99.99 ~4×, mean ~20 %. Max still has kernel\npreemption events (311 µs / 66 µs with pinning vs 3.7 ms without —\neven preemption is rarer on pinned cores). Interestingly,\n`0,12` (SMT siblings) and `0,1` (independent cores) are nearly\nindistinguishable for this latency-bound round-trip, suggesting\nSMT helps hide channel-wait latency rather than hurting via\nresource contention.\n\nSingle-step bump 0.3.3 → 0.3.4."
}
```

**Result of 0.3.4 work** (measured on 3900X, `mpsc-2t -d 10`):

| pinning | mean | stdev | p99.99 | max |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| none | 7,044 ns | 6,545 ns | 74 µs | 3.7 ms |
| `0,1` | 5,636 ns | 1,321 ns | 17 µs | 311 µs |

stdev ↓5×, p99.99 ↓4×, mean ↓20%. SMT-sibling variant nearly indistinguishable from independent cores for this latency-bound bench (SMT hides channel-wait latency).

**Proposed Checkpoint-1 commits for 0.3.4:**

App repo:
```
jj commit -m "feat: --pin CPU affinity flag (0.3.4)" \
  -m "Add --pin CORES flag for pinning bench threads to logical CPUs,
reducing scheduler migration noise and keeping TSC reads on a
single CPU across start/elapsed. CORES accepts comma lists with
range shorthand (e.g. 0,1 / 0-5 / 0,0) and is treated as a pool
indexed positionally with wrap-around, so thread i pins to
pool[i % pool.len()]. This naturally handles oversubscription
and scales to future N-thread benches.

New deps: core_affinity 0.8. New module src/pin.rs with parser
and thread-pin helpers (7 unit tests). RunCfg gains pin_cores
slice and a core_for(idx) helper. main() pins the measurement
thread before calibration so framing overhead is measured on the
same CPU used for the bench. mpsc_2t pins its worker on spawn.

Measured effect on 3900X mpsc-2t -d 10: stdev ~5x tighter,
p99.99 ~4x tighter, mean ~20% lower. README documents the flag
with SMT-sibling (--pin 0,12) vs independent-core (--pin 0,1)
examples and the topology-check command.

ochid: /.claude/xxoqnqxurwyz" -R .
